# Agent records campaign plan

## Goal and exit criterion

Make `@orkestrel/agent`'s two long-conversation strategies durable and honest, give `AgentContext` memos and skills in the package's own vocabulary, gate tool calls with deterministic checks in the loop, and keep the `system` message host-authored, adding nothing the evidence does not carry.

- Fixes: blank-recap refusal, owner-trim order, ledger head pricing, literal vocabulary, the host-authored `system` message (records in the request turn, workspace text in a context message), and the layout vocabulary its reading keeps.
- Capabilities: ledger snapshot and hydrate, memo and skill managers wired into the context and the ledger's agent options, Authority `ask` with the `consequential` floor, and pair narrowing as the measured option `LedgerCorrectionOptions.narrow`, default false. The correction cap leaves the list by the user's word of 2026-11-03 (O74, O161, W4).
- Experiments, each promoted or deleted on its written gate: the moving tail, deleted as `c7e1d12e` on its failed gate (O163). Pair narrowing left this list by the user's word of 2026-11-03: its gate is kept on O164's reading and it ships as an option (O161, O164). Anchored recaps stopped at their L0 gate and are deleted unbuilt (O32, O75).
- Measurement: every gate reads on the smallest local model the Study carries, because the smallest models are the most sensitive to these changes and Agent must be usable by them without workarounds (Q5).
- Exit: every unit accepted cheap-first; every experiment switch either promoted (switch deleted, behavior always on) or deleted with its bench, except `LedgerCorrectionOptions.narrow`, which the user's word of 2026-11-03 re-classed from an experiment switch to a measured option under T57 and T58 and which meets this clause as that option (O161, W2); on every path Agent emits exactly one `system` message, built from host inlets only, proven by the canary test; Agent 0.0.35 published with the guide corrected; no `src/` line whose justification is "an experiment might test it".
- Open wording question (O165, Y22), the user's: the exit reads "exactly one `system` message" on every path, and `AgentContext#build` emits none when no host inlet holds text. Fable's reading is "at most one, and exactly one when an inlet holds text", which the canary test proves on every path, and the falsify round tests claim 5 on that reading. The exit's text stays as written, because a re-baseline never moves an exit criterion.

The campaign builds no second log, no CRDT, no frontmatter parser, no default literal extractor, no core repeat-stop switch, and no guard model.

## Decisions

Each decision is the user's. The user ruled Q1 to Q29, Q31, and Q32: Q1 to Q15 on 2026-10-31, Q16 to Q29 on 2026-11-01, Q31 on 2026-11-02, and Q32 on 2026-11-03; Q30 is ruled on the user's stated trade-off and listed for the user to veto. The user's standing rules of 2026-11-01 on keeping a measured improvement that misses its target and on the campaign's scope, and their standing word of 2026-11-02 on further runs, are quoted verbatim, recorded, and applied by the Orchestrator rulings O64, O69, and O116.

| --- | --- | --- |
| Q1 | The noun: `Memo` or another word (`Fact` collides with the `fact` category, `Note` with `LedgerNote`) | `Memo` |
| Q2 | Whether the Study compaction arm with the authority repeat rule becomes the compaction baseline | yes; every experiment's off arm runs on 0.0.34, and the 0.0.30 c1 arm is not comparable |
| Q3 | Whether the memo and skill managers ship in Agent 0.0.35 with the `studies/context` mechanism study as their only consumer, the toolbox tools following | yes; the toolbox tools wait for D20. Authority `ask` ships in 0.0.35 as rc-a14 under the user's ruling of 2026-10-31 (O2) |
| Q4 | The GPU allowance in model minutes, and whether the recording extends to goal 77 | left to the Orchestrator: 600 model minutes split per step, and the recording extends to goal 77 (O9, O17, `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance) |
| Q5 | Whether a hosted-provider consumer is planned | no; Agent is built for the smallest local models, which expose a design flaw where a larger model works around it (O10, `designs/measurement.md` § Models) |
| Q6 | Whether Agent 0.0.35 may stop a `consequential` tool from running in an application with no approver | yes ("approved on `consequential`"): no fleet tool sets the annotation (F6), and Agent ships no compatibility shim (`@orkestrel/scaffold` `AGENTS.md`, no compatibility shims; `designs/authority.md` § Default authority and the consequential floor) |
| Q7 | Whether a Study release, 0.0.9, carries rc-st1 and, when rc-a9's reading needs it, rc-st2, and when it is published | yes ("work on the release as needed"): the campaign's Study release is 0.0.9, carrying rc-st1 and rc-st3, proven on CPU fixtures and published before rc-a9's first segment; rc-st2 stays conditional and ships in Study 0.0.10, published before the first records screen, when rc-a9's first records segments project a records screen over its cap with a live judge (`designs/runs.md` § Progress) |
| Q8 | Whether each GPU lineage freezes its Node 22 patch: the most recent Node 22 release is read at each lineage start and held to the lineage's last replay, amending O18 for the window | yes (approved), because positional replay refuses any other Node release, a patch included (`designs/runs.md` § Replay and pins) |
| Q9 | Whether each GPU lineage freezes its Ollama version: the most recent stable release is screened at a lineage start, and every arm of a gate runs under one daemon pin that the profile `daemon` member makes Study verify | yes (approved) (`designs/runs.md` § Replay and pins) |
| Q10 | Whether one CPU test lane may run beside a GPU segment | no (D47; the user ruled as recommended); if granted, only for gates that read tokens and labels, never rc-a3 or rc-a11, after rc-p4 reads within 5% of quiet |

| Q13 | The browser and server boundary, round 1: whether 0.0.35 refuses a stored `system` role at every ingress (D33); whether one `Agent` shared across concurrent requests is guarded or only documented; who owns the system turn a relay forwards; and whether the page-to-Node placement is named with a wire contract | refuse the stored role: "Refuse it now (Recommended)"; the shared agent: "Let's think this one through, I feel that might be the point is to be able to switch in and out on a dime, but if it means a security issue or if it weakens the agent or makes it brittle instead of helping with freshness then think it through"; the relay: "Not sure but I would say the server should always win but if the user wants the server to accept a system prompt then let them but explicitly otherwise the page to server prompt can either silently drop or explicitly error out, but the point is to be explicit between them."; the placement: "Name it and ship a contract" (O34, O37) |

## Rulings

### Verified facts

- F1. The Ollama judge sends one request per question: the `OllamaJudge` remarks in `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.27 read "Each question uses a separate non-streaming generate request." Grouping a message's asks saves zero wire calls, so the grouped-asks experiment (the subjective proposal's E3) is refused.
- F2. The owner-record trim drops the most recent lines first: `Ledger#plan` builds owner-record steps from `record.lines` in conversation order, and each `Ledger#trim` step replaces the keyed record's lines with `record.lines.slice(0, -1)` (`src/core/ledgers/Ledger.ts`, as of `1e82d145`). The rules record survives only because `selectRecords` moves on-topic rules lines ahead of off-topic ones. rc-u5 is a fix, test first.
- F3. `Message.id` never reaches the Ollama wire: `mapMessages` in `@orkestrel/ollama` 0.0.27 sends `role`, `content`, `tool_calls`, and `images` only. The system-message id that `AgentContext#build` mints on every call leaves Ollama prefix reuse intact. Probe rc-p1 (c) is dropped, and D17 closes.
- F4. `@orkestrel/tool` declares `ToolAnnotations { pure?, untrusted?, consequential? }`: `untrusted` "Reports that the tool's value can carry content the tool did not author"; `consequential` "Reports that running the tool has a consequence a caller must confirm" (`ToolAnnotations` in `@orkestrel/tool`, https://github.com/orkestrel/tool).
- F5. `@orkestrel/mcp` maps `consequential` to MCP `destructiveHint` and to WebMCP `consequentialHint` (`toolAnnotationsToMCP` in `src/core/helpers.ts` and the browser mapping in `src/browser/helpers.ts`, https://github.com/orkestrel/mcp).
- F6. No `@orkestrel/toolbox` source sets `untrusted` or `consequential` (https://github.com/orkestrel/toolbox).
- F7. `@orkestrel/ollama`'s `tests/service/authority.test.ts` builds decisions with `allowed`, so its re-pin migrates that file (https://github.com/orkestrel/ollama).
- F8. `ToolManager#execute` in `@orkestrel/tool` starts a batch with `Promise.all`, so rc-a14 rebuilds the allowed map in call order.
- F9. `raceSignal` exists (`src/core/conversations/helpers.ts`, as of `2da6d0b6`): it removes its abort listener on settle and absorbs a late rejection, so the ask pass uses it and adds no abort helper. `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/patterns.md` § Event maps forbids a generic status event, so decisions publish as `allow`, `ask`, and `deny`.

### Orchestrator rulings

- O1. Adopt the consultation's decision, reconciliation, records, compaction, memos and skills, context and machinery, measurement, sequence, and deferrals, with F1 to F3 applied. The `designs/` files hold that substance.
- O2 (restated 2026-10-31). `remember` never ships without `ask`, and `ask` ships first, in Agent 0.0.35 as rc-a14; D6 closes. The user ruled on 2026-10-31: "we still need to address Authority and ask ... security really has become an issue with models and I want to make sure we have that integrated and ready to work as soon as possible and working well."
- O3. Route each `astra` unit to the objective bench when the roster lists that bench as available; when it does not, the unit runs on the subjective lane's engine and the carrying commit records the substitution.
- O4. The noun is `Memo` (Q1).
- O5. Stop line: after Agent 0.0.34 is served and pushed, the campaign runs every unit from S1 through S8 without further user input, each GPU step inside its cap (O9). It stops and reports before publishing Agent 0.0.35, because the user owns every publication (`tenets.md` § Release) and every push except that of a release the registry confirms (O31). It also stops and reports when the GPU allowance is spent.
- O6. Estimate: no measurement of these units exists. The closest is the prior campaign's R2 CPU proof, which ran 327 of 335 implementation minutes for one medium unit (`152ac3c3:.orkestrel/agent/r2-u41-ruling.md`). Each unit brief states its own estimate and stop cost.
- O7. The closed fleet records `.orkestrel/plan.md`, `.orkestrel/ledger.md`, `.orkestrel/release.md`, and `.orkestrel/scaffold/release-audit-verdict.md` record closed campaigns (fleet 2026-10-17, Mechanisms, Recording, the 2026-10-24 release, the boost campaign), and the fleet wave confirmed on 2026-10-30 that it reads none of them. The records commit deletes them per `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/campaign.md` § Hand off and close; git history keeps them.
- O8. These records are committed by explicit pathspecs; the first records commit, `30c8cc71` on 2026-10-31, left the uncommitted tarball pins in `package.json` and `package-lock.json` out of the commit. Nothing in this campaign pushed Agent's `main` before `main` was pushed through `fe5a1703`, Agent 0.0.34's release commit, after the registry served 0.0.34.
- O9. GPU allowance (Q4): 600 model minutes, the summed `total_duration` of every agent and judge call, split into the per-step caps of `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance. A cap stop is a budget stop, never a quality verdict (`tenets.md` § Scope). The recording extends to goal 49 on both arms and to goal 77 on the records arm.
- O10 (amended 2026-10-31). Subject model (Q5): every GPU gate reads on Qwen 3.5 2B, the smallest model with prior readings; the records judge stays Mica v0.1 4B; an experiment that promotes passes one non-inferiority pair on Qwen 3.5 4B (`designs/measurement.md` § Models). The user confirmed the 2B subject on 2026-10-31: "Sounds good, we can start with the 2b model."
- O11. The `system` message carries host-authored text only: nothing a user wrote, a tool returned, a judge or summarizer produced, or a model wrote, a model-written memo included. Agent's inlets to it are the system text, instructions with loaded skill bodies, the skill index, and host-registered tool definitions; the tools block renders inside the `system` turn (`designs/layout.md` § Boundary). The user ruled on 2026-10-31: "we need to be careful and make sure that nothing gets into it from the user like we found that it has, and also after it we need to make sure that we have the proper order of text that goes to the agent from the context and in ways that the agent can digest it effectively."
- O12. The layout move, rc-u10, is a fix with no switch. Its cost is read one time, by rc-a10 for rc-u10 against a Study transport rewrite that rebuilds the 0.0.34 placement; that arm is study-only and deleted after the screen, and no screen returns records to `system` in what Agent ships. Amended by Q30's ruling of 2026-11-02: that read is one time or recorded unread, and the rewrite is deleted either way (`designs/measurement.md` § Screens).
- O13. The Study pins the effective sampling values on every role; a value change is D38 (`designs/layout.md` § Sampling).
- O14. Adopt the security consultation, with F4 to F9 and C32 to C41 applied; `designs/authority.md` holds that substance. Its ruling that lookup readings stay in the `system` message and its form of D25 are superseded by O11 and O15.
- O15. Adopt the layout consultation, with C42 to C47 applied; `designs/layout.md` holds that substance, and § Sequence holds its merged unit table.
- O16. `AuthorityInterface.ask` is optional, and the `Authority` class always implements it. A later move to a required `ask` is breaking; the reverse is not.
- O17. The GPU allowance stays 600 model minutes, split again per `designs/measurement.md` § GPU allowance: rc-a6's 90 minutes fund rc-a16 (15), rc-a10 for rc-u10 (45), and its contingency (30); rc-a15 takes 10 from the larger-model checks, which keep 100.
- O18. Node line (the user, 2026-10-31: "Let's have everything run on the latest 22 version going forward unless the package requires 24."): every check, gate, probe, and study run of this campaign runs on the newest Node 22 release at the start of the run, recorded in its run record, and on the newest Node 24 release at the start of the run, recorded in its run record, only for a package whose manifest requires 24. The declared support floor stays `engines` `>=22.18.0`.
- O19 (amended 2026-10-31). Run shape, on the user's ruling of 2026-10-31 that no model run is ridden out (`designs/runs.md` § Ruling): every GPU execution is one arm and one sealed segment, run from a `study freeze` closure, started from an end-of-goal checkpoint a sealed earlier segment staged (a lineage's first segment from goal 1), and projected at 15 model minutes or less from the last sealed segment's measured rates; the first segment of rc-a9's lineage holds 2 goals, the second 6, and a screen lineage opens with its `on` arm's first segment of `designs/runs.md` § Segment plan. Each decision other than a Study ceiling is taken at a segment boundary by launching or not launching the next segment. A step's cap is enforced by projection before launch; the `record` allowances sit at twice the projection as a backstop that must never bind, because a `record` refusal leaves the result interrupted (Study `guides/study.md` § Native command, the recording-allowance paragraph; `designs/runs.md` § Run shape).
- O20. Stops: Study ceilings and Agent ceilings keep their layers; an invariant failure (more than one `system` message or one not at index 0, a missing or changed pinned sampling key, `think` not false, a model digest off the pin, a closure fingerprint or Node runtime differing across a gate's arms) ends the chain as "measurement invalid"; curtailment ends only a treatment arm, only on a count clause the gate has already lost, only after its control arms sealed; every other signal is an alert read at the boundary. No in-process quality guard ships in the first lineage (D39; `designs/runs.md` § Stops).
- O21 (amended 2026-10-31). Progress: a progress reader parses `exchanges.jsonl`, `evidence.jsonl`, and `attempt.json` by byte offset at segment completions only. The reader is Study's `study progress` command (rc-st1), in Study 0.0.9 served before rc-a9's first segment, and it calls the memory adapter's `progress` (`StudyMemoryAdapter#progress`, rc-a17) per listed goal. rc-p3 is retired and its id never returns. The term is `progress` (`designs/runs.md` § Progress).
- O22. Fixes while a step runs climb the ladder fixture, hydrate, replay, request, micro, screen (`designs/runs.md` § Fixes while a step runs). Writers edit and run owned-file format, lint, and type checks beside a live segment; tests, builds, offline replays, and other model work wait for the boundary. § Parallelism carries the GPU line.
- O23. The `rc-o` prefix the run-operations designs proposed is not used: the probes are rc-p2 to rc-p4, the seed and checkpoint instrument is rc-a17, and the Study units are rc-st1 and rc-st2. The README id law admits any `rc-` id and names the `rc-st` Study kind.
- O24. The `q2` profile's goal call bound is 512: bursts average about 137 judge calls (`research/evidence.md` § Prior-campaign readings, judge bursts), and a 128-call goal bound was exhausted at goal 49 in the prior campaign (§ Prior-campaign readings there, goal call bound). Its sampling, `record`, `observe`, and `daemon` members follow `designs/runs.md` § Run shape and rc-s1's first change in § Sequence, and its every-goal checkpoint list follows rc-a17 there.
- O25 (amended 2026-10-31). The GPU table keeps O17's caps (sum 600) with the segment plan of `designs/runs.md` § Segment plan. When a screen's projection exceeds its cap with a live judge, take the judge saving from rc-st2 when Study 0.0.10 is served (Q7), else cut goals per arm and report the stratum reached; the contingency and larger-model rows stay reserves.
- O26 (amended 2026-10-31). Studies layout, adopting the studies-organization consultation (rc-consult5) on the user's ruling of 2026-10-31, "make the studies folder nice and organized and any tmp folder we should come up with for generated stuff we shouldn't need to track", as the naming consultation (rc-consult6) amends it under O29. A declaration folder holds only `study.json` and `rubric.json`. `studies/capacity` and `studies/qualification` keep their names: `capacity` is the capacity twin of `memory` over `handoff`, `qualification` is the capacity twin of `endurance` and produces the result the profile member `qualification` names, and no single word replaces either without inventing a term. `studies/memory/` holds the driver code, `profiles/` (`local`, `q4`, `q2`, and one single-word profile per screen arm or experiment switch as it lands), `scenarios/`, `provenance/{endurance,handoff}.json`, and `sources/{README.md,manifest.json,evaluation.json,development.json}`; the README states custody facts only, and `provenance/endurance.json` alone holds the rubric's derivation. `studies/memory/profiles/q4-room.json` is removed, and the `q4` profile's `qualification` member names `tmp/studies/qualification`. Results, closures, gradings, labels, and run records live under `tmp/studies/`; no generated file sits under the tracked tree, and no `.gitignore` line is added. rc-s2's `studies/context/` enters as one declaration over the `tools` driver: `study.json` and `rubric.json`, its `libraries` scenario under `studies/tools/scenarios/`, the shared `local` profile, and `tests/studies/context/integration.test.ts`. rc-u12 carries the move.
- O27. Answer key: no live unit, test, script, or configuration reads the three files under `studies/endurance/sources/private/`; the custody rule bars them from every prompt, query, label, and selection rule; the rubric derived from the evaluation oracle is tracked on its own; and lane searches of the tracked `studies/` reached that directory (the search deviations of `ledger.md`). The files leave the tracked tree by the Orchestrator's directory rename to `tmp/studies/private/endurance/`, with their bytes unread and never copied, before rc-u12's builder starts; on a rename refusal the Orchestrator stops and reports and never copies. `studies/memory/sources/manifest.json` stays tracked and keeps their SHA-256s, and git history holds them at rc-u12's parent commit, `64da0a29`. `tenets.md` § Evaluation's never-read line names the history location and the untracked copy. No history rewrite.
- O28. Sequence: rc-u12 runs in the runner lane right after rc-u1 and rc-u2 are accepted and committed, before rc-p1, rc-a8, rc-a14, and any later baseline that reads `studies/` or `tests/studies/`. This departs from the consultation's "before rc-u1 and rc-u2": those two units read and write no file under `studies/` or `tests/studies/`, and their baselines (2026-10-31, `f8ea2662`) were taken on the unchanged tree.
- O29 (amended 2026-10-31). Naming and ignores (the user, 2026-10-31: "let's try to keep file names as single words, specifically the JSON ones, I don't like the kebab case, and also if it has to be an ignored file or folder then it goes into the tmp folder I don't like gitignore rules that cut into nested folder and files, that's confusing." and "Also, it's okay to have kebab case named files for generated files or files in the tmp folder but not tracked files and folders."): tracked JSON files take single-word names, as `host.json` does; no tracked file or folder takes a kebab-case name except where a convention requires it (skill directories named `orkestrel-<word>`); files under dot folders (`.claude/`, `.agents/`, `.orkestrel/`) are exempt; a TypeScript class file keeps the PascalCase class name of `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/names.md` § Files and folders (`StudyMemorySession.ts`); kebab-case is free for generated files and anything under `tmp/`; the user refined the rule on 2026-10-31 in the scaffold session, which carries it into the scaffold law ("it is mainly for JSON files similar to how we have host.json, I still don't want kebab named files or folders in general but if it is a requirement of convention like how it is for skills then that is the narrow exception"; "You can exempt the files in the dot folders"); an ignored file or folder lives under `tmp/`, and no `.gitignore` pattern reaches into a tracked folder.
- O30 (amended 2026-10-31). Segment selection and seed custody live in Study 0.0.9 through rc-st3, adopting the naming consultation (rc-consult6); Study 0.0.9 is proven on CPU fixtures and published before rc-a9's first segment, and the rc-s1 re-pin moves Agent to it in S6: `study check` and `study execute` gain `--trial <name>`, `--goals <first>..<last>`, and `--seed <result>:<trial>:<goal>`, recorded in the result manifest and verified by custody (`designs/runs.md` § Run shape). Study refuses a closure run under a profile other than the one it froze (`StudyCommand#closure` in `@orkestrel/study`, "Study closure selectors differ from the checked selection", https://github.com/orkestrel/study at `e319242`), so one closure is frozen per lineage per arm profile and serves every segment of that arm in that lineage; a fix between segments opens another lineage with its own closures. A screen arm is a declaration trial plus a tracked single-word profile. Every declaration Study reads sits under the consumer root's `studies/` with no link, so with the tracked tree closed to generated files the only other home is a scratch consumer root under `tmp/`, which copies every admitted package root per lineage and binds the recorded identity to a copy; that route is the fallback D49, not the design.
- O31 (amended 2026-11-01). Pushes (the user, 2026-10-31: "it's okay to commit locally on main but don't commit and push to origin until we are sure and especially are ready to publish"): every campaign commit, record or unit, stays local on `main` until the user and the Orchestrator are sure of the work and it is ready to publish; the push goes to the user at that publish point with the list of commits it carries. On 2026-11-01 the user ruled that a release the registry confirms is pushed at once: after the registry serves a version the user published, the Orchestrator pushes that checkout's `main` through its release commit without a further go-ahead, as `@orkestrel/ndjson` 0.0.15 was (§ State). Agent's `origin/main` stays at `ad145075` until Agent 0.0.35 is published, and no push ever rewrites a remote branch.

Both server fences scope threads to the principal (`designs/boundary.md` § Run binding, § Page wire): the application's session table holds one entry per authenticated principal, `{ agent, threads }` for rc-u14's Node fence and `{ agent, threads, approver }` for rc-u16's wire fence, where `threads` is a `createConversationManager()` value that principal owns and `agent` is one `Agent` built once per principal from shared providers, tools, rules, and immutable host text. No fence looks up a client-facing thread id in a process-global manager: rc-u14's fence becomes `handle(session, threadId, text)` over `session.threads`, and rc-u16's frame route uses `session.agent`, because a per-request `Agent` leaves the per-agent `'CONCURRENCY'` guard unreachable (D52 stays deferred). The guide names what a per-principal `Agent` shares across its threads: the workspace pointer, the scope, instructions, memos, the emitter, and `agent.abort()`. Each session entry builds its own `window`, `budget`, and `allowance`, never shared across principals, and while one run is live an `Agent` built with any of them refuses that principal's next run on any thread with `AgentError` `'CONCURRENCY'` (409 on the wire, a rethrow in Node) rather than queue it, so the host bounds a run it cannot finish; the application owns the entry's lifetime and eviction. rc-d1's inlet table binds the thread id to the principal and keys a durable store by principal (§ Units, row rc-d1). Each fence transcription adds two cases: two principals posting one thread id get distinct conversations, and a same-principal overlap on one thread answers 409 (wire) or rethrows (Node) with the thread's message count unchanged. Each fence keeps the message `thread.add` returns, wraps only the synchronous `stream` or `generate` call, calls `thread.remove(message.id)` on any synchronous throw, then answers 409 for `AgentError` `'CONCURRENCY'` (wire) or rethrows (Node), and removes nothing on an asynchronous rejection (`designs/boundary.md` § Run binding); a run that appends its own user turn is D64. The ask id stays minted by the `Approver`, and the call-object correlation stays, because a loop-minted id frames asks nothing waits on, turns a redacting wrapper from fail-closed into a disclosure of the unredacted arguments, and turns D58 into a race (`designs/boundary.md` § Page wire, Rejected). Instead, `agentStreamToFrames(stream: AgentStreamInterface, approver: ApproverInterface | undefined)` takes the approver as a required positional parameter, so omitting it fails to compile and passing `undefined` is a visible choice whose TSDoc says ask chunks are dropped; the guide adds that a wrapper that redacts or copies the call gets no frame and waits until the run `timeout`, when one is set, or until the page disconnects and the route's `cancel` aborts the run, and that redaction belongs to D60 (`designs/boundary.md` § Page wire). An operator policy over relay `options` is D65, and rc-d1 adds one sentence that a relay forwards `options` and the history under the operator's credential (`designs/boundary.md` § Deferred). rc-u14 tests the ledger's `'REQUEST'` refusal by conversation identity, not message id, and the claim covers the selection only: a run on another conversation still reaches the ledger's `recall` and lookup tools and, during a `respond` call, its gauge readings and repeat stop, which the guide states (`designs/boundary.md` § Run binding). Both server fences remove the user turn only on a synchronous throw, so a run that settles partial or rejects before any assistant append leaves it as the thread's tail; both designs name it (§ Run binding, Opened or left open; § Page wire, Opened or kept). The frame route composes the same origin and CSRF checks as the answer route before it reads the body (`designs/boundary.md` § Page wire, Route pattern). rc-u16 launches after rc-u14 and rc-u15 both commit (the Orchestrator's launch condition, 2026-11-01), superseding O44's "rc-u15 can continue beside rc-u16"; rc-u15 can continue beside rc-u6. No default run `timeout` ships: every Node consumer would change, and disconnect wiring bounds the wire case (`designs/boundary.md` § Page wire, Opened or kept). O45 supersedes the third boundary round's rulings that kept the advertised-name guard to the `ask` verdict and gave its `deny` event the evaluated decision (`designs/boundary.md` § Review). The user ruled on 2026-11-01 that `createRelay` keeps refusing every undeclared page tool definition by default (Q16), so rc-u15 builds § Relay ownership as approved and the consumer migration line of § Release names the tools option beside `system.accept`, and that `RelayProviderOptions.parser` becomes optional with an opt-in strict mode of `@orkestrel/ndjson` as its default (Q17, rc-nd1 and rc-u17).

A refusal ends the stream: the refusing call returns nothing, discards every line it holds before and after the refused one plus the partial line, and leaves the parser as `clear()` leaves it, so a caller that keeps feeding gets another `'SYNTAX'` on the dropped line's remainder, never a spliced record; the guide's opening paragraph says the lines after the refused line in the same call are discarded, and the `strict` sentence sits in the `NDJSONParserOptions` remarks. `errors.test.ts` stays, because the guard is logic; the deep-nesting case probes the host engine and asserts against the measured result; the distribution case that needs the registry can skip before the release and is required under `--mode release`; Signature cells are copied from the `test:guides` output. rc-nd1 launches with rc-u14 and rc-u15 on the `ad866d2b` and ndjson `699230c` baselines, as the one writer in the `@orkestrel/ndjson` checkout; it runs only its owned-file format check, non-fix lint, and scoped type checks, and the one runner lane runs every test, mutation, and gate. Strict mode trims each line with `String.prototype.trim` in both modes, with no untrimmed strict variant: rc-nd1's trim case iterates the ECMAScript whitespace set and the line terminators a split line can hold, each row written with `\u{…}` escapes on its own strict parser, and rc-u17's default-refusal case places the trimmed settled-result line after an earlier complete line, because `TextDecoder` strips a byte-order mark at byte 0; the guide says the change is Unicode space around a line inside the stream. The `'PROTOCOL'` translation is framing-agnostic: private `#parse(parser, chunk, combined)` and `#finish(parser, combined)` in `AgentProvider`, called where `parser.parse` and `this.finish(parser)` run, rethrow the error unchanged when `combined.aborted`, `isProviderError(error)`, or `isProviderAbortError(error)` holds (brand guards, never `instanceof`) and otherwise throw `ProviderError('PROTOCOL', 'provider error: malformed wire record', { cause: error })`; neither encloses `this.read(record)`, `#emit`, or the loop body; `#translate` and its lead comment stay; `AgentProvider.ts` imports nothing from `@orkestrel/ndjson`; the class remarks gain the framing-seam sentence and the guide's `'PROTOCOL'` bullet covers a line the framing refuses. Its cases: a plain `Error` from `parse` or `finish` becomes `'PROTOCOL'` with that error as `cause`, a `ProviderError` `'PROVIDER'` from a custom parser passes through, `isNDJSONError(error.cause)` holds on the default relay path, and the mutation deletes the wrap. Both server fences keep removing the user turn only on a synchronous throw and add no tail check, because a tail check needs both exits (a cancel or a run timeout resolves) and erases an ask whose partial answer the page might already show; `designs/boundary.md` § Run binding records the rejection, and D64 is the route that closes the gap. The kept tail is a stated limit with an executed proof: rc-u14 adds a server-section case (a scripted provider that rejects before the first append; `handle` rejects; the tail is the appended user message; a second `handle` on the same thread appends after it; and the second run's recorded request carries both user messages adjacent and in order), rc-u16 extends its cancel-during-ask case the same way, and each lands its sentence with its case: a run that rejects, aborts, or times out before any assistant append leaves the user turn as the thread's tail; the next request carries it unanswered directly before the following turn, so the model can answer it or call tools for it, and a call the authority allows outright runs without asking; an application that wants a cancelled ask withdrawn calls `thread.remove(message.id)` after settle while that message is still the tail. `designs/boundary.md` § Page wire dates its "no SSE or NDJSON package" clause at `ad866d2b` and names rc-u17's dependency. rc-u17 runs late, with no interim Ollama or Study release: it launches after the campaign's last Study command that loads a profile's Ollama provider entry (every execute and every offline or keyed replay under `q2`, `q4`, and each rc-s1 per-arm profile, the `q4` larger-model checks of `designs/runs.md` included) and after every unit whose acceptance names `test:studies` (rc-s1's last profile change or re-pin, and every strip or removal unit for an experiment switch or profile), immediately before rc-d1 in S8. Its criterion 0 is that `npm ls @orkestrel/ndjson --all` lists 0.0.15 at the top level and exactly one nested 0.0.14 under `@orkestrel/ollama`, and nothing else; `study check` is no evidence for the nested copy, because `check` excludes the provider entry. - O53. Measurement method (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on a measurement plan, a host survey, a research lane, one read-only consultation, and one refuter, adopting the consultation's amendments and the refuter's). The user delegated the measurements on 2026-11-01: "I'm leaving most of the measurements up to you, since they are subjective and difficult for me to gauge except as a user of the packages in an application. I need you to thoroughly research these measurements and also check the computer we are using and make sure that they are reasonable measurements while also still trying to get the best performance we can." Thresholds and methods are therefore Orchestrator rulings, and only scope and product behavior go to the user. Every gated reading from rc-a3b on follows this method, which `designs/measurement.md` § Method holds in full.
  - Statistic: wall time is primary; CPU is read only where a cell's median is at least 500 ms, because the CPU timer's quantum is 16 ms. One process per arm per cell, arms interleaved, garbage collection unforced, warmups before measured samples, and tinybench with `time: 0`, `iterations: n`, and `warmup: false`. Every ratio gate at or under 1.10 runs at least 2 processes per arm, interleaved A B B A, and every 1.05 gate at least 3, because the t multiple at 2 processes, 4.30, leaves a 1.05 gate about 1% of margin; at 5 or fewer processes, a t interval on process medians replaces a cluster bootstrap. A ratio gate reads both builds interleaved in one slot, the before build from a copy the Orchestrator takes at that slot's start beside the after build and the A/A copy; an earlier before reading is a trigger reading, a cross-check, or a sensitivity control only (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-6).
  - Controls: an A/A bound at 2 to 2.5 times the measured between-arm noise, a slot whose A/A trips reported as unreadable, apart from pass and fail; a sensitivity control that injects the threshold plus 3 times that noise and also the threshold itself, which reads at least marginal; and before any gate at or under 5% is set, one A/A pilot of at least 6 processes on the 1600-message seed fixes the between-process noise and rewrites the detectability table; the pilot runs over the control `select` first in rc-a3b's before slot, and rc-u19's first measurement repeats it over the correction `select` (O54).
  - Host preconditions per slot: no `node`, `npm`, `tsc`, `vitest`, or `oxlint` process outside the runner's tree with a CPU-time delta over 100 ms across the slot, read from the process list at slot start and end, each such process present before the first slot, the IDE's background daemon, and the network service recorded by command line as known background under their own bound (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, HP-1), and a slot whose A/A or outlier rule trips rerun; GPU memory in use at or under 100 MiB and the Ollama process count at the daemon plus its tray, read at slot start, at slot end, and per cell, never contacting a daemon; other load at most 6.25% over a 10 s quiet baseline before the first cell and at most 10% per cell, as hard bounds, with 3% as the aim, a cell over 10% rerun one time and a second miss reading `contested`; a reference kernel (a 200 ms spin-and-sort) at slot start and end within 3%; the power plan recorded by its identifier; identical V8 flags across arms, with `--max-old-space-size` unset.
  - Thresholds: rc-a3b's gates read wall time with CPU beside, and a control non-regression gate joins them, the after build's control arm at 1600 messages at most 1.05 times the before build's (O54 sets rc-a3b's own acceptance); rc-u21's cost trigger falls to 12.5 ms under the page rule at the end of this bullet, its slope trigger needs at least 4 ms at 20000 deltas, the smallest page-path gain worth having, so it fires on growth before the total reaches 12.5 ms, its pace-keeping case reads within 1.05 times, and it reports the largest single append at 20k beside the total; rc-u20 reports the 1804-judgment control seed's hydrate at most 1.05 times beside its gate, and its upstream guard sites join at 12.5 ms under the same rule; rc-u19's drop is at least 3% and at least 16 ms, read on the frozen rc-a3 seed for both builds, and rc-u19 is not built when rc-a3b's after-run correction arm reads under about 530 ms; rc-ct1 keeps 20%, its A/A built from two separate copies of the HEAD build and bounded under the method's A/A rule; the floor of Q26 stands as the user ruled it, the control arm's `select` CPU at 1600 messages with wall time beside, its slope on the span from 800 to 1600 messages; after rc-a9, the reading at the realistic size rc-a9's report names and `select` wall as a share of the 2B time to first token rc-a9 reads are reported beside it, and gating the floor there comes to the user as a proposal, because it changes Q26's basis; every 1.05 non-regression gate runs at least 3 processes per arm; every `src/core` path that can run in a page uses the page budget divided by a 4x device factor, 12.5 ms on the measuring host, the factor's source recorded in O55.
  - Lane hygiene: every brief that can run commands forbids the `ollama` CLI, `ollama --version` included, and any contact with port 11434 or 11435, because the research lane ran `ollama --version`, which contacts the server to print its version; probes stay inside the checkout's probe directory or its unit directory, never in a folder outside the checkout.

  - rc-a3b's own acceptance: on its committed bench, the correction arm's wall slope from 800 to 1600 messages at most 2.0, with the marginal rule of `designs/measurement.md` § Method stated before the before run; replay byte-identical; and the after build's control arm at 1600 messages at most 1.05 times the before build's, at 3 processes or more per build, both builds interleaved in the gating slot (O53). Q23's 2x correction-to-control bar at 1600 messages is read jointly after rc-u19 lands, because the cost left after rc-a3b is rc-u19's terms, the cached pair reads and the copy of every judgment read; when O53 cancels rc-u19 (the after-run correction arm under about 530 ms), the bar is read right after rc-a3b. A 2x miss after both feeds rc-a18's ruling on the absolute floor (Q26), which opens at most one more bounded ledger unit. This supersedes the 2x gate of O51 and O53 as rc-a3b's own acceptance, and clears the pre-launch hold the rc-a3b brief carried.

- O56. rc-a3b's acceptance (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on rc-a3b's before, cost-only, and after slots, a read-only re-evaluation of its slope miss with one refuter per proposal, its review, and the user's Q27; `designs/measurement.md` § Method, TH-2). The after slot reads the correction arm's wall slope from 800 to 1600 messages at 2.032 [2.019, 2.045], df 4, `misses` against the bar the user set in Q23 (at most 2.0). Every record keeps that reading, and no bar is re-derived for the slot: a bar derived from the operation count and a 2.1 tolerance were refused as set after the data, because `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Performance sizes the threshold before the run. The repair is kept as a stated override, under that section's rule to keep an algorithmic repair the measurement shows and under the brief's miss clause, which reverts "as the Orchestrator rules", on this evidence: the correction arm at 1600 messages from 124418 ms to 2133 ms (58x) and at 800 messages from 15723 ms to 522 ms (30x); the cost per cached judgment read from 1214 and 2444 µs, which doubled with the message count as O51's cubic rescans did, to 40.3 and 41.9 µs; the control arm at 1600 messages 0.148 of the before build's (198 ms against 1340 ms), `meets`; the A/A passing, S `marginal`, and T `misses`; every digest byte-identical within the slot and equal to the before and cost-only slots'; and the host preconditions held. Diagnostics, recorded beside the miss and deciding nothing: the cached-read count slope 1.974 (12956 reads at 800 messages and 50906 at 1600, equal on the before build); the wall slope on process means 1.966 and the CPU slope on process medians 1.967; two of seven 800-message samples about 95 ms high in each process, a garbage-collection bimodality; and the per-read residual over the control-arm base, 34.2 then 38.0 µs, about 11% per doubling, a residual slope of 2.13. The tallies alone do not classify the residual: it is the feature's own quadratic (each correction paired in `Classifier#gather` with every earlier non-quiet message sharing a near topic, and `classification()` walking every recorded judgment per selection) plus a per-operation drift of 4% raw or 11% over the control base whose cause (cache, garbage collection, or a mild per-pair term) is unmeasured. The cost-only slot kept the alias index (a gain of 1133.5 ms, the log difference's 95% interval [0.396, 0.454]) and not the plan hoists (−27.9 ms, [−0.046, 0.020]), which the lane reverted, so `#projectLines`, `#buildUnitRecord`, and `#collectAmended` equal the launch commit's and `#plan` differs only by the `LedgerScan` it passes down. The review accepted with four minors, each closed in a follow-up: the report states this ruling, a `measureOtherLoad` case proves that CPU spent inside the run leaves the load unchanged, the `buildLedgerIndex` case pins which word needles compile, and the hoist claim reads that no plan hoist remains and the three methods equal the before build.

- O60. rc-u21's controls (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on rc-u21's after slot; `designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-17). The first comparison group read `unreadable`: S, a nominal 5.0% spin, delivered +2.36% and read `meets`, and T, a nominal 7.4%, delivered +5.13% and read `misses`, each read on the median of process medians. The instrument was sound; a spin in the paced case overlaps part of each round's waiting time, about 0.05 to 0.10 ms, and delivers less than its nominal size. Both controls were recalibrated to effective sizes from that group's own medians, S = 0.05 × 3.8608 + 0.0958 = 0.2888 ms and T = 0.075 × 3.8608 + 0.0958 = 0.3853 ms, and the group reran one time as the `unreadable` rule's rerun; the first group's A/B, 1.022 [0.99975, 1.0452], is context only. The loss read in that group, 0.055 to 0.097 ms, did not recur in the reruns, where S delivered +7.6% and +7.4% of the before build's median against its 5.0% target and T +9.5% and +10.5% against 7.5%, so the loss is no constant: a later brief measures it in the slot that uses it and never carries rc-u21's figure over. The lane's departures stand: the after slot ran as three groups, the comparison group gating and the generation and shape groups reported only; the generation row's warmups rose from 2 to 30 for MM-7's 1 s floor on the after build's 16.8 ms call; and the bench timeout rose from 60 to 120 s. One read-only `git show` by the lane is a recorded deviation.
- O61. rc-u21's acceptance (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on rc-u21's comparison groups, its review, and the re-read of its final build; `designs/measurement.md` § Method, TH-4). The recalibrated rerun at 3 processes per build read readable and `marginal`, A/B 1.0309 [0.9909, 1.0726], and the doubled rerun at 6, fixed before the run, read `meets`, 1.0235 [1.0057, 1.0417], A/A −0.0106 [−0.0306, 0.0095], S and T `misses`, c_diff 0.0089, other load at most 3.0% per process. The repair is kept. Its remark "a taken cell is released at once" was false for the newest taken cell, which `#tail` holds until the next push, and the text was made true, not the code; the launched brief's amendment-3 answer stands as the report restates it. The review found that a reader suspended at its `yield` held its taken cell, whose `next` chain kept every later cell reachable, a retention the copied array never had; the drain loop reads the value and moves `#head` before the `yield`, so no cell outlives its take in a reader's frame, the remark states the bound (a channel retains at most one taken value, and a suspended reader holds only the value it took), the mutation files follow the loop, and the after build was recaptured. The re-read of the committed build at 6 processes per build read readable and `meets`, 1.0329 [1.0187, 1.0473], A/A 0.0025 [−0.0149, 0.0198], S and T `misses`, c_diff 0.0073, other load at most 4.6% per process, so the committed build is the measured one. The paced case, a reader keeping pace with the pushes, pays a measured 1.9% to 4.7% (point 3.3%) for the linked cells, beside generation over 20000 deltas about 81x faster (1074.9 ms to 13.2 ms, ratio 0.0123 [0.01165, 0.01299]), pushes with no reader linear (case A's slope 0.98 against 3.03 before, case C's 1.06 against 2.58), and the largest single push at 20000 deltas 0.005 ms against 4.2 ms. The mutations' exact failing sets hold within `Channel.test.ts`.

The independent review accepted with two minors: the fixture finding is fixed by a `meter` and a `model` finish row, a refused `layer: 'other'` row, and refused prompts with a numeric image and with no content, and the report's mutation row carries the eleven proven results. - O64. The keep rule (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, recording and applying the user's standing rule of 2026-11-01: "If something misses a target by a bit but still had better performance, we should keep it and not revert it.", read against `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Performance on the records check of the wave 1 apply). A change is kept when its measurement shows it faster on the case it targets, the interval clearing no change in the faster direction, even when the point misses its improvement target, and the miss is recorded as read, with no bar moved after the data. A reading that shows no gain, its interval including no change, reads not shown, and keeps the change only when its brief names a merit beyond speed before the run: correctness, bounded retention, or a simpler reading for a developer or a small model. A pure speed change whose gain is not shown reverts, because `quality.md` § Performance refuses a micro optimization without its own measured gain. A change that makes a case users hit slower beyond its no-regression bar reverts in every case, and a mixed change, faster in one case and slower beyond the bar in another, reverts or goes to the user, as rc-ct1's did (Q28, O62). `tenets.md` § Scope holds the rule, and `designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-18 states how a brief applies it. Every brief not yet launched (rc-u19, rc-u20, rc-u6, rc-u18, rc-a18, and every later performance unit) has its miss and revert clauses rewritten to this rule in a brief-2 before launch; O56 already applies it to rc-a3b.
- O65. No local adoption of rc-ct1's walk (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on the ct1 local-adoption lanes, a read-only consultation, and one refuter per proposal). No site in Agent or a related package adopts the span walk, and contract's source stays at the 0.0.21 bytes with the pins and the bench of `09d0673`. The mechanism, read from the builds and the slot records and not ablated: the walk saves about 34 ns per step (no generator resumption, getter and closure, `Reflect.apply` with a fresh array, or second result object) and adds about 0.43 to 0.51 µs of setup per loop, so it wins only on long walks over cheap elements; the crossover, about 13 to 15 steps per loop, is inferred from the measured loss (1 to 16 entries, 5 to 8 keys) and gain (1000 or more), not measured. Agent holds no array that is both large and cheap-element: its large arrays (messages, sections, judgments) run heavy element guards, and the 51000-judgment pool through a prebuilt `recordOf`, the analogue of Agent's judgment hydrate, read 19% slower. In Agent, an index loop is refused by `@orkestrel/scaffold` `.claude/rules/typescript.md` § Bindings and a local walk variant by `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Ecosystem reuse, because it carries no semantic difference, and it would drop contract's pinned-prototype guarantee. The fleet survey found no hot site that runs a container guard over a large cheap-element array. A later contract revisit first adds size cells of 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, and 128 to `tests/src/core/combinators.bench.ts` and reads the crossover in one interleaved process pair against the released build and the rc-ct1 candidate build, kept untracked in the contract checkout's probe directory, or a later candidate. A figure carried from the contract bench to Agent illustrates and is never an Agent measurement.
- O66. rc-u20 is the lever (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on the ct1 local-adoption round). The cost is guard construction per call, not the walk: `isJudgment`, `isSection`, and `isMemo` build a `recordOf` or `objectOf` on every call (U3, about 7 µs per construction under the profiler, an upper bound), and rc-u20 owns the hoist. rc-u20 launches after rc-u19 commits, only on a brief-2 written to O64 and checked: a hydrate drop on the correction seed that its interval shows, short of 25% or 250 ms, is kept with the miss recorded; a drop that is not shown reverts, because the hoist is a pure speed change, unless the brief-2 names a merit beyond speed before the run; the control seed's hydrate over 1.05 times before reverts a site in every case; and its write trigger takes its own reason or is dropped. No gain figure is cited for rc-u20 except its own before and after readings. A hydrate reading folded into rc-a18 is dropped, because rc-a18's profile reads only marked regions, every hydrate falls outside them, and its slot is not ordered after rc-u20.

The cached tally, 12956 reads at 800 messages and 50906 at 1600 on both builds (O56), counts only `#ask`'s cached readings and is no copy count. When no m up to 6 reads 3%, m is fixed at 6 as a stated departure from `designs/measurement.md` § Method, MM-9, a contingency, in place of stopping the unit. In place of MM-13's 3.0% clause, the slot reads `unreadable` when (q + 0.84) × c_diff(m) × M at the slot's re-derived c_b exceeds the forecast drop, so the slot can still show the forecast drop against no change. The after slot reruns one time, at 2m when the 3% target reads `marginal` or when the point sits under 1.0 with the interval including 1.0, and an `unreadable` slot's rerun is that one rerun; a second `unreadable` slot reads not shown. A shown gain keeps the change, with `meets`, `marginal`, or `misses` against 3% and 16 ms recorded as read; a gain that is not shown reverts it, because rc-u19 is a pure speed change and its brief names no merit beyond speed. A failed no-regression bar or a differing digest reverts it in every case, and a failed bar beside a shown gain is a mixed change the brief rules a revert. Each no-regression bar reads its point only, so no `marginal` reading arises on one. - O88. The Data-Oriented Design read (Orchestrator ruling, 2026-11-01, on a read of Richard Fabian's *Data-Oriented Design*, a read-only consultation with proposals G1 to G8, and one refuter per proposal, each proposal as its refuter amended it). G8, G4, and G3 are working rules binding this campaign, cited to the book's node 9 and node 4: `designs/measurement.md` § Method holds them, and `tenets.md` § Briefs holds one line.
  - G8: a unit that adds or changes a pass over messages or judgments states the pass's in and out cardinality (filter, one-to-one, or emission); an emission states its bound and where it applies, naming a host-set or absent bound as such; a bound on a model-paid emission applies before the call; and the unit states whether it removes a superlinear term or a constant factor.
  - G4: a new loop over messages or judgments ships a deterministic pin of what it asks of an observable boundary (judge questions, host callbacks) at the layer that does the work, preferring an exact read history; a count is a screen and decides no keep; only a brief claiming a speed change on a store states its reads per write, each marked counted or derived; a stated forecast sits beside its reading as one line, and a ceiling is never scored as a miss. No retrofit runs over sites `dd8dee79` already pins.
  - G1: a deterministic pre-filter before a paid judge call is sound only when it is conservative for everything the paid step can file. The literal veto does not move, because `amends` on a literal-free pair is the paid gate to a supersession, pinned by `asks pairs using whole names and call arguments, and requires tokens only for amends marks` in `tests/src/core/ledgers/Classifier.test.ts`. One throwaway offline probe, with no model and no GPU, runs scripted corrections at the burst goals and reports eligible asks, literal-free asks split by what admitted them, registry-id asks, and the fileable pairs rc-a11's set would miss, as rc-a11's forecast. rc-a11's brief carries one user question on literal-free `supersedes` filing, stating that a Study replay cannot answer it.

The user answered on 2026-11-01 that `select` runs on server and page (Q29, O90), so the rule is written before the run against the server budget, 660 ms for `select` and hydrate per request, with the page served by rc-u22's yield (`designs/measurement.md` § Method, TH-8). While the reading sits under that budget, D67 and D75 do not open on a share trigger alone, and stress sizes read growth for those two. TH-3, Q26, O57's D12 routing, D76, rc-u19, and rc-u20 stand. The rule lapses when a re-read at rc-a9's realistic size, or after a unit changing `select`, crosses the budget.

G2: no cross-request filing table is recorded as D12's shape, because the book's direction is calculable over cached (node 9: "poor form to add state and complexity for the sake of performance until late"), so when rc-a18 shows the read side worth spending on, D12 tries one side-effect-free pass per `select` on the existing `LedgerScan` first, and a cross-request table is proposed only on a repeated-select reading at the page budget with answers to `assign`, the write route, the freshness inputs, store-order replay, and the copy-on-write law; `#plan` and `#recall` reading `input.classification` is a stateless simplification that claims simplicity, about 3% of reads. G5: no standing store-or-derive rule, because `designs/records.md` already says it better; D79 survives. G6: guards are public contracts and the payoff is about 10 ms. G7: no row narrows the repeated question and state, because they are what make the stale check work and a narrower row has no V8 read gain; rc-a9 reports the share of snapshot bytes in `state` and `question` beside its checkpoint bytes.

  - The path is a source reading. Qwen 3.5 renders through the daemon's Go renderer, and a template applies only to a model with no renderer (`server/prompt.go` and `model/renderers/qwen35.go` at the Ollama tag v0.40.13; C42, C43). The local config is inferred, because the registry manifest's hash differs from the receipt's digest.
  - Reach, on that source reading and unmeasured: every rendered string is a carrier, on every role. Content on each role passes `mapMessages` byte for byte, and the renderer writes a replayed call's name and each argument key raw. On the page-to-server path `createRelay` refuses a page `system` message and an undeclared tool definition by default, and `messageShape` admits `calls` on any other message, so a page can write a call name or an argument key that fabricates the system turn the relay refuses. Stored ledger text replays through the same path.
  - What a token gives one untrusted author on a small model: a turn boundary the model was trained on, structurally the same as the host's own system turn, so an instruction to treat tool text as data has nothing to hold, a prior turn or an approval can be fabricated in the context, and D31's delimiters would be defeated. What it does not give: a `consequential` call still stops at the gate, because the gate reads no model text (`designs/authority.md` § Protection). Agent never protected the model's choice or its answer, and plain text already moves a 4B model 44.6% of the time (C35). The token raises the ceiling of injection, not its floor.
  - The rule, worded to the mechanism: from 0.0.29 the Ollama provider escapes the tokens in its published list, which covers Qwen's role, turn, tool, and reasoning tokens, on every string it sends that the daemon renders, on every role, the host's `system` text included. Other vocabulary tokens and other model families are not neutralized: `MICA_SPECIAL_TOKENS` holds 13 strings against 33 added tokens in the published Qwen 3.5 2B tokenizer config, none of the missing ones a role boundary, and other families' markers are absent. The list stays unchanged and unrenamed. Uniform escaping leaves the wire making no trust decision, so a host that pastes or loads text into `system` cannot make that mistake (`tenets.md` § Trust), and the daemon's renderer supplies the structure, so no legitimate text needs a raw token.
  - Neutralization reuses `escapeSpecialTokens`, a U+200B after the opening `<` of each listed token. Ordinary text keeps identical bytes, and a request holding a listed token changes by one U+200B per occurrence. A false positive costs boundedly: text that spells a listed token gains an invisible character, which a model can copy into a `write` call. The judge path has accepted that cost since Ollama 0.0.27.
  - Where: `mapMessages` in `@orkestrel/ollama` `src/core/helpers.ts` escapes `message.content`, `call.name`, and each argument key and top-level string value. `format`, `options`, `images`, and `model` do not render into the prompt and stay untouched, and `types.ts` does not change.
  - Measured before built: `qwen35.go` writes tools and nested values through `json.Marshal`, whose default turns `<` and `>` into escape sequences, so one live count arm for a tool description and one for a nested argument read first. When both read as text, the `encode` change and the recursion drop, and a template-rendered family stays a stated limit extended when the package tests one where the arm collapses. ## Sequence

Every unit starts after Agent 0.0.34 is published and pushed and designs against its names. Size `S` is small, `M` is medium; `run` is a GPU run. Each medium unit gets one independent review. "Gate row" points at `designs/measurement.md` § Gates. A unit that adds, renames, or removes a barrel export owns that symbol's `guides/agent.md` rows, because `test:guides` asserts that the guide documents every barrel export (`tests/guides.test.ts`); rc-d1 owns the guide's prose and the strategy-choice table.

### Phases

| Phase | Step | Units |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | layout and probes; rc-u12 runs after rc-u1 and rc-u2 are accepted and committed, and before rc-p1 (O28) | rc-u12, rc-p1 |
| S2 | fixes and the trust boundary | rc-u1, rc-u2, rc-a8, rc-u5, rc-a14, rc-u10 |

a judgment or a message written during a pair-phase await read at the next `classify` call, where 0.0.34 read it at the next rescan of the same call, and a host's `LedgerLookup.read` run one time per tool message for each `#plan` call, `#recall` call, and `select`'s classifier pass, where 0.0.34 ran it again at every rescan, which a host whose `read` has side effects or counts calls observes (rc-a3b, O51; `designs/records.md` § CPU cost and memoization); `JudgmentManager` reads, `add`, and `resolve` returning the stored deep-frozen record where 0.0.34 returned copies. The same frozen records are the judgments that `ConversationInterface.snapshot()` and `LedgerInterface.snapshot().conversation` carry, `MemoryConversationStore.get` hands those snapshots back by reference, and a classifier result whose one resolved judgment supplies `usage` carries that record's frozen `usage` object. So a write to a returned judgment throws in strict-mode code, and so does a host's edit of a snapshot's judgment to migrate or redact it (rc-u19, O51, O54, O55, O70, O89); a judgment record whose `usage` member is present with the value `undefined` refused by `isJudgment`, `isConversationSnapshot`, `isLedgerSnapshot`, and `requireConversationSnapshot`, where 0.0.34 accepted it; JSON never carries that value, so it reaches only a host that passes an in-memory snapshot holding one (rc-u20, O78); and a throw from a provider's framing seam (its parser's `parse` or the provider's `finish`) that races no cancel and is not already a `ProviderError` or `ProviderAbortError` rejecting as `ProviderError` `'PROTOCOL'` with that throw as its `cause`, where 0.0.34 passed it through unchanged, while a throw from `read`, `frame`, or `clear` is unchanged (rc-u17, O47). `AgentContextInterface` gains the required members `skills` and `memos`, so a host's own implementation of the interface adds them; a context from `createAgentContext` or `createAgent` is unaffected, and no related package implements it (rc-u9). `LedgerInterface` gains the required method `snapshot()`, so a host's own implementation adds it (rc-u4).
